Nero Delhi, the 25th January 1956 . 

NOTIFICATION 
No.M II - 152 (269 ) 53 :-In exercise of the powers 
conferred by sections of the Mines and Minerals (Regula 
tion and Development) Act, 1949, (LIII of 1918) the 
Central Government hereby makes the following amend 
ment in the Mineral Concession Rules, 1919, namely : 

In clause (u ) of rule 3 of the said Rules , after the 
word " gravel" the words " Chalcedony pebbles" shall be 
inserted. 


( Sd .) R. N. VASUDEVA, 
Deputy Secretary to the Government of India .

NOTIFICATION 
Whereas it appears to the Government that the lands Specie 
fied in the Schedule below are needed for a public purpose 
stated below notice to that effect is hereby given to all whom it 
may concern in accordance with the provisions of Section 3 (1 ) 
of the Cochin Land Acquisition Act it of 1070 as continued in 
force by the Travancore-Cochin Administration and Application 
of Laws Act VI of 125 and the Government hereby authorise 
the District Collector Trichur his staff and workmen to exercise 
the powers conferred by section 3 (?) of the Act. Under 
Section ? (c) of the said Act the Government appoint the 
District Collector, Trichur to perform the functions of the 
Collector under Section 4 (a) of theAct.

17th March 1956. 
L. A. ADDITIONAL LANDS Kurrur BRANCH CANAL IN 

- Section | Block 5 Peechi Scheme. 
Whereas it appears to the Government that the lands 
specified in the schedule below are needed for the public 
purpose stated below , notice to that effect is hereby given to 
all whom it may concern in accordance with the provisions of 
Section 3 (1) of the Cochin Land Acquisition Act, Il of 1070 , 
as continued in force by the Travancore-Cochin Administration 
and Application of Laws Act, VI of 1125, and the Government 
hereby authorise the Special Land Acquisition Officer, Irriga. 
tion Schemes, Trichur, his staff and workmen to exercise the 
powers conferred by Section 3 (2) of the Act . Under Section 
2(e) of the said Act the Government appoint the Special Land 
Acquisition Officer, Irrigation Schemes, Trichur to perform the 
functions of the Collector under Section 4-A of the Act.

(Chief Secretariat-Home Section ) 
DEVOLUTION OF POWERS TO THE INSPECTOR 

GENERAL OF POLICE. 
Read : -ROC. No. 327 /GA /S dated 5-8-1955 from the 

Inspector General of Police. 
ORDER K. Dis. No.22510/55/CS DATED 2ND APRIL 1956 . 

Government are pleased to order that the powers as 
detailed in the annexure will be exercised by the Inspector 
General of Police for the efficient day-to-day administration 
of the Department. 

By order of His Highness 

the Raj Pramukh, 

S. K. SARMA, 

Deputy Secretary to Government. 

ANNEXURE . 
3: Appointments and promotions. To make any non-gazetted 
appointment on the sanctioned ministerial establishment of 
his office or of the offices subordinate to him , the pay or the 
maximum pay of which does not exceed Rs. 150 per mensem 
subject to the Public Service Recruitment Rules. 
2. Disciplinary powers. 

() To grant all kinds of leave to all officers according 
to rules except the District and Assistant Superintendents of 
Police, subject to verification by the Comptroller. 

(1 ) To transfer all officers except the Assistant Super 
intendents of Police and the District Superintendents of Police 
and the Deputy Inspector General of Police. 

(111) To sanction the journey on duty of all officers 
except the Assistant Superintendents of Police, District Super 
outside the State , 
3 . 

Finducial Powers . 

() To sanction purchase from P.W.D. Workshops 
of furniture and permanent fittings required for the Department 
subject to budget provision 

(1 ) To sanction purchase of games articles, sports 
implenients, accessories etc. subject to budget allotment. 

The power should be exercised subject to Store Rules . 

(tt) To sanction advertisement charges up to Rs. 50 in 
each case, subject to the rates being approved by the Director 
of Public Relations. This has already been delegated to all 
Heads of Departments in G.P. D.Dis. 24084 / 55/Fin. dated 
5-10-1955. 

(iv) To write off unserviceable articles including 
damaged and worn out appliances, apparatus, clothing articles 
etc. when the book value does not exceed Rs. 250 in each 
case. 

() To dispose of unserviceable articles when the book 
value does not exceed Rs. 250 in each case, subject to the 
rules in Article 175 and 476 read with Article 173 Financial 
and Account Code. 

( 1) To sanction the sale by, suction of all standing 
and fallen trees unserviceable buildings and plants in grounds 
under the control of the Police Department, subject to the 
proviso that when unserviceable buildings are sold, survey 
reports should be got prepared by the P.W.D.and then sanctionel. 
If such sale involves the write off of Rs. 2000 and above, 
sanction of Government should be taken for sale . 

(vii) To grant leases of trees on the Government lands 
under his control subject to the rules for the time being in 
force regarding lease of Government lands. 

(vhit) To sanction the shifting of an office, or checking 
post from one building to another and the hiring of buildings 
on rent for the above purpose in case of necessity when the 
rent in each case does not exceed Rs. 75 per mensem subject 
to builget provision. The rent of the building will be subject 
to the production of rent certificate from the Rent Controller 
or from the P.W.D. authorities as the case may be. 

(ix) To sanction the auction sale of usufructs of trea 
and the lease of premises for cultivation of the office of the 
Inspector-General of Police and those under the control of the 
Police Department.

Whereas, the Government are of opinion that an Industrial 
Dispute exists between theManagement of Messrs.Peirce Leslie & 
Co., Ltd., Cashew Factory, Kundara represented by the Manager 
and the Workmen of the above concern represented by the 
Travancore-Cochin Watchers and Peons Association , Quilon 
in respect of matters mentioned in the annexure to this order, 

And , whereas, in the opinion of Government it is necessary to 
refer the said Industrial Dispute for adjudication 

Now therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by Section 
10 (1) (c) of the Industrial Disputes Act 1947 (Central Act 
XIV of 1947)the Governmenthereby direct that the said industrial 
dispute be referred for adjudication to the Industrial Tribunal, 
Trivandrum .

6. In selecting the candidate for the award the Head 

master shall be guided by marks and the poverty 
certificate produced by him . But before the School 
requisites are awarded the selection should be got 

approved by the Director of Public Instruction . 
7. In making the selection , a pupil who is already in 
receipt of such 

or similar educational concession 
shall be excluded. 
8. The amount shall never be paid in cash, but such 

books and other school requisites as the pupil 
requires shall be purchased by the Headmaster with 
the amount available and handed over to the pupil 
after taking a receipt from him showing the details 
of articles received by him . The Headmaster should 
submit to the Director of Public Instruction 
vouchers for the articles purchased for the award 

as well as pupil s receipt mentioned above. 
9. If it is not possible to supply all school requisites 

with the annual income the Headmaster is compet 
ent to purchase and supply such of the books and 
articles as are absolutely necessary and that could 

be purchased with the amount available. 
10. The pupil who is in receipt of the school requisites 

is forbidden from transferring the whole or part 
of the articles or books supplied to him either by 
sale or otherwise till he completes his Upper 
Secondary Department Course. He should produce 
the articles to the Headmaster whenever he is 
called upon to do so. If it is proved that the pupil 
has misused the award and thus destroyed the 
purpose of the donor, the Headmaster is competent 
to get back such of the articles or books as are 

in the possession of the pupil and cancel the award. 
11. If in any year the school requisites are not awarded 

the amount of the annual income shall be invested 
when there is sufficient amount for such investment. 
The annual income accruing from such investment 
shall also be utilised for the award of the school 

requisites contemplated in clause 5 above . 
12. If any doubts or disputes arise as to the meaning 

or interpretation of the above rules, it shall be 
referred to the Director of Public Instruction 

whose decision shall be final. 
13. This scheme shall come into operation from the 

date of publication of this Notification in the 
Gazette.

NOTIFICATION 
R.Dis.No.2344 |56 |EHL . 

26th March 1956. 
In exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section 
( 1 ) of Section 4 of the Charitable Endowments Act , 1890 
(Central Act VI of 1890 ) the Government of Travancore 
Cochin hereby order that the property mentioned in 
column (2 ) of the Schedule appended herewith belonging 
to the Endowment mentioned in column ( 1 ) thereof shall 
be vested in the Treasurer of Charitable Endowments for 
Travancore-Cochin and under sub section (1 ) of Section 
5 of the said Act Government settle the following 
scheme for the administration of the said property , 

SCHEME 
1 The Endowment shall be called " The Sivarama 

krishna Iyer Memorial Prize Fund " . 
2. The Headmaster, S.M.H.S. Thathamangalam shall 

be the administrator of the Fund. 
3. The amount of Endowment of Rs. 300 /- (Rupees 

Three hundred only) offered by Sri T. S. Rama. 
chandra Iyer B.A.(Hons. ) Thathamangalam shall 
be vested in the Treasurer of Charitable Endow 
ments, Travancore Cochin and the amount of 
interest realised will be utilised for the award of 

prize. 
4. The amount of the Endowment shall be invested 

in any long term securities of Government of India 
or in any other securities, approved by the Govern 

ment 
5. The annual income accruing from the Fund shall 

be utilised for the award of school requisites at 
the re -opening of schools, to a poor and brilliant 
student of Form IV, S.M.H.S. Thathamangalam . 
(a ) In the above clause the word " School 

requisites" mean text books, note books and 
writing materials as may be required by the 

pupil consistant with the amount available . 
(b ) The word "poor" means the pupil who in the 

opinion of the Headmaster, is poor. 
( c) The word " brilliant" means a pupit who shows 

the best intellectual attainments as shown by 
the marks obtained by him in the Form III 
Common Examination .

NOTIFICATION . 
No. I. 1-23023/55 / DD , 

3rd April 1956 
It is hereby notified that the packing charges 
for cement for the period commencing April first 
to June thirtieth will be at the flat rate of 
Rs. 13-4-0 per ton. This charge is applicable to 
cement in new bags only Charges for cement in 
old bags will be settled between the buyer and 
the seller, but should not exceed Rs. 13-7-0 per 
ton .

NOTIFICATION. 

6th March 1956 
Whereas the Government have directed the 
Survey of the lands comprised in Survey Nos. 
noted below of Irooppara Village, Trivandrum 
Taluk it is hereby notified under Clause (1) of 
Section 6 of Act X of 1094, that Survey operations 
will be started in the above Village and the 
Survey Nos .noted below of the above said Village 
will be demarcated and surveyed; and that every 
person claiming to be interested in the registered 
lands situated within or adjoining the above 
mentioned lands is hereby invited to attend 
immediately either in person or by agent on the 
Surveyor employed in the locality and also from 
timeto time when called upon , for the purpose of 
pointing out the boundaries and supplying infor 
mation in connection therewith . 

Under Clause (2 ) of Section 6 of the above 
said Act, this notification shall be held to be a 
valid notice to every person having any interest 
in the abovesaid lands. 

Under Clause (3 ) of Section 6 ofthe above 
said Act, all the registered holders are hereby 
required : 

( 1) to clear within a week by cutting down or 
removing any trees, jungle, fences, standing 
crops or other materialobstructions, the bound 
aries or other lines, the clearance of which may 
be necessary for the purposes of Survey and 

(2) to provide labour at such times and for 
such periods as may from time to time be re 
quired for furnishing flag-holders and chainmen ,

1955 மார்ச்சு 19 . 

டுறவு சங்கம் ஆர்பிட்டிறேஷன் , எக்ஸிகியூஷன் அன்ட் 
ஈடாகவேண்டும் துகைவிவரம் - திருவனந்தபுரம் கூட் 

லிக்கியுடேஷன் டெப்பூட்டி ரெஜிஸ்திரார் ஆப்பீஸில்

- டுறவு சங்கம் ஆர்பிட்டிறேஷன் , எக்ஸிகியூஷன் அன்ட் 834-ாம் நம்பர் விதி நடத்தின்படி 747 ரூபா 13 அணா 
விக்கியுடேஷன் டெப்பூட்டி ரெஜிஸ்திரார் ஆப்பீஸில் 4 பைசா 949-ாம் நம்பர் கல்குளம் கூட்டுறவு தாலூக்கு 
( பயில் 1957 - ல் 1021-ாம் நம்பர் எவார்டின்மேல் 1954 - லெ

பாங்கி ( லிமிட்டெட்டற்கு கொடுத்து தீர்க்க வேண்டி 
6 பைசா 949-ாம் நம்பர் கல்குளம் கூட்டுறவு தாலூக்கு 

யிருந்தது அடைத்து தீர்க்கப்படாததால் ஷெ விதிநடத்து 
பாங்கி ( லிமிட்டெட் ) ற்கு கொடுத்து தீர்க்கவேண்டியி 

அனுசரித்துள்ள 120 ரூபாயும் லேலத்தியதி வரையுள்ள 

மேல் பலிசை பிழையும் நடபடிச் செலவுகளும் உள்பட 
ருந்தது அடைத்து தீர்க்கப்படாததால் டி விதிநடத்து 
அனுசரித்துள்ள 300 ரூபாயும் லேலத்தியதிவரையுள்ள 

ஆகெ முதலான 826 ரூபா 14 அனா 10 பைசா . 
மேல்பலிசை பிழையும் நடபடிச் செலவுகளும் உள்பட 

உாம் பிரதி - கோ தநல்லூர் வில்லேஜில் கிராமம் தேசத் 
ஆகெ முதலான 321 ரூபா 1 அணா7 பைசா . 

து ஸ்ரீவிலாலந்து வீட்டில் ஈசுவரபிள்னா ராமன்பிள்ளை. 
2 -ாம்பிரதி- வாள்வச் சகோஷ்டம் வில்லேஜில் மருதூர்க் 

வஸ்துவிபரம் .

16th March 1956. 
It is hereby notified that the lease right of 
cultivation under taungya system and planting of 
cashew in the 21:25 acres selected for opening 
cashew plantation in Kummil Reserve in Nedu 
mangad Range during the year 1956 will be re 
auctioned by the Divisional Forest Officer Trivan 
drum at his headquarters at Trivandrum on 
11-4-1956 . The auction will commence at 3 P. M. 

2. Intending bidders should deposit a sum of 
Rs. 100 (One hundred) only . before the auction 
commences withoutwhich nobody will be allow 
ed to bid . 

3. The period of the lease will be two years 
from the date of confirmation of the sale . The 
contractor will be allowed to raise agricultural 
crops such as paddy or gram or other cereals in 
the leased area for which he must remit a lease 
rent at the rate of Re. 1 per acre per year. No 
tapioca cultivation is allowed . 

4. The cost of planting cashew will be given 
to the lessee by the Department.* After the ini 
tial planting is over the lessee will be paid 75 % 
of the cost of the planting provided the work is 
done satisfactorily . The nce 25 % cost 
of planting and the cost of subsequent casualty 
replacement etc., will be paid only after the com 
pletion of all works and the area handed back to 
the Department. 

5. Subject to the approval of the Chief Con 
. servator of Forests the work will be confirmed in 

the name of the lowest bidder provided his cha 
racter and financial status are found satisfactory, 
bụt the Divisional Forest Officer reserves to him 
self the right to accept or reject any bid assigning 
any reason . 

6. The successful bidder will have to remit a 
security at the rate of Rs. 15 per acre and execute 
an agreement on one rupee stamp paper within 
15 days from date of communication of the con 
firmation orders. If the successful bidder fails to 
remit the security and execute the agreement 
within the period allowed above he shall have to 
pay penal interest at 12 % on the security for a 
further period of 15 days. In the event of further 
failure the right will be resold at his risk and 
loss forfeiting the amount already paid to Govern 
ment but he shall have no claim over the gain if 


any , by that sale. The agreement would be exe 
cuted within 15 days from date of remittance of 
security. 

7. Other general conditions of the agreement 
to be executed by the successful bidder can be 
seen in the Divisional Forest Office, Trivandrum 
during office hours and the bidders are expected 
to get acquainted with the said conditions before 
participating in auction , 

8. Further particulars can be had from the 
Divisional Forest Office, Trivandrum or Range 
Officer , Palode.

3. Only one percentage of the basic rate for 
each should be quoted . 

4. The lowest tender would ordinarily be 
accepted provided the character and the finan 
cial status of the tenderer are found satisfactory. 
The Divisional Forest Officer reserves to him 
self the power of acceptingor rejecting any tender 
without assigning any reason therefor. 

5. Acceptance of tender is subject to the 
approval of the Chief Conservator of Forests or 
Government as the case may be . The successful 
tenderer cannot withdraw his offer before final 
decision ofthe competent authority . 

6. The successful tenderer will have to remit 
a security amounting to 10 % of the total cost 
including earnestmoney already remitted,within 
15 days from date of communication of sanction . 
If the tenderer fails to remit the security within 
the time allowed , he will be given another 15 days 
time with interest at 12 % per annum . He will 


also have to execute an agreement on an one 
Rupee Stamp paper within 15 days from date of 
remittance of security . 

7. Violation of any of the above conditions 
will entail cancellation of the contract besides 
forfeiture of the earnest money, deposit besides 
making him liable for any loss Government may 
sustain by making other arrangements for carry 
ing out the work . Any amount thus becoming 
payable by the tenderer will be made good by 
Revenue Recovery Act in force or other mean s . 

8. The earnest money chalan of the unsuccess 
ful tenderer will be returned with repayment ) 
order for encashment atthe Treasury . 

9. Period allowed for completing thework 
One month from date of agreement. 

10. No. of trees noted in the schedule is only 
approximate The tenderer cannot have any 
claim for compensation if the number of trees fail 
short of that noted in the Schedule while amounts 
will be paid on the excess trees marked at rates 
quoted by him .

22nd March 1956 . 
1. Sealed tenders are invited for raising one 
acre elavu nursery in Kulathupuzha Range as 
per particulars shown in the schedule given 
below . 

2. Tenders in the usual form should be ad 
dressed in sealed covers to the Divisoinal Forest 
Officer , Quilon Division , Punalur, so as to reach 
him in his Office at Punalur before 3 P. M. on the 
20th April 1956. They will be opened by the 
Divisional Forest Officer or any other Officer duly 
authorised by him on his behalf at 3 P. M. on the 
same day in the presence of such of the ten 
derers as are present. 

3. The Tender should be accompanied by a 
chalan receipt of having remitted an amount of 
Rs. 60 into Pathanapuram Sub- Treasury under 
revenue deposit on behalf of the Divisional 
Forest Officer. Quilon as earnest money . The 


receipted chalan for earnest money of the un 
successfultenderers will be returned immediately 
with repayment order on disposal of the tenders 
and that of the successful tenderer adjusted to 
wards security for the work . The necessary. 
chalan for the remittance of earnest money can 
be had from the Divisional Forest Office, Punalur. 

4. In the tender, only one percentage of the 
basic rate given below should be quoted both in 
words and figures. The percentage will apply to 
all the basic rates . Tenders which quote more 
than one percentage will be rejected . 

5. The lowest tender would ordinarily be ac 
cepted provided the character and financial status 
of the tenderer are found satifactory ; but the 
Divisional Forest Officer reserves to himself the 
power of accepting or rejecting any tender with 
out assigning any reason therefor. 

6. Acceptance of the tender is subject to the 
approval of the Chief Conservator of Forests. 
The successful tenderer cannot withdraw his 
oller before finaldecision from the Chief Conser. 
vator of Forests .

SHENCOTTAH DIVISION . 
NOTIFICATION 

22nd March 1956 . 
The right of collection and removal of tree 
growth as detailed at foot, from the 50 cents of 
land forming part of survey No. 1/3 B of Patha 
napuram Pakuthy (The land to be transferred to 
the Education Department) will be auctioned by 
the Divisional Forest Officer, Shencottah in his 
office at Shencottah subject to the following 
conditions: 

1. The auction will be conducted at 3 P. M. 
on 26th April 1956 . 

2. Persons interested in the purchase may 
inspect the area and satisfy themselves about the 
extent and nature of tree growth therein . No 
complaints will be entertained after the auction . 

3. Persons wishing to take part in the 
auction shall deposit an earnestmoney of Rs. 50 
before the auction commences. The earnest 
money will be returned to the unsuccessful 
bidders as soon as the auction is over. The 
earnest money deposited by the successful bidder 
will be adjusted towards the 1/3 value which he 
has to pay on the spot. 

4. The sale will be ordinarily knocked 
down to the highest bidder provided his character 
and financial status are satisfactory . The Divi 
sional Forest Officer, reserves to himself the 

right !o sccept crucect any bid without assign 
ring any reason. The sale is subject to confi 
mation by the Chief Conservator of Forests. 

5. The highestbidder shall have to abide 
by the orders of the competent authority and 
shall not withdraw from the offer till receipt of 
final orders . 

6. The highest bidder shall remit the 1/3 
of the full value of tree growth bid by him 
immediately after the auction is over. If he fails 
to remit the 1/3 value as stated above, the sale 
will be cancelled and the tree growth will be 
reauctioned at his risk and loss. 

7. When the sale is confirmed by the 
competentauthority the purchaser shall deposit 
a sum as security amounting to 10 percent of the 
total sale value and shall execute an agreementon 
one Rupee stamp paper within 15 days from the 
date of intimation of the confirmation of the sale . 
If he fails to deposit the security within the time 
specified an extension of 15 days time will be 
given for the remittance of the security subject 
to recovery of 12 per cent interest per annum . If 
the purchaser fails to remit the security within 
the period of extension allowed the sale will be 
cancelled and the tree growth will be reauctioned 
at his risk and loss. The 1/3 .amount already 
paid by him will also be forfeited to Govern 

8. The purchaser shall remit the balance 
2/3 value of the tree growth within 15 days from 
the date of intimation of the confirmation of the 
sale . 

9. If he fails to remit the balance value 
within the time specified in condition 8 above an 
extension of 15 days time will be given for the 
remittance of the balance amount subject to 
recovery of an interest at the rate of 12 per cent 
per annum . 


10. If the purchaser fails to remit the 
balance value within the period of extension 
allowed as per condition 9 above the sale will be 
cancelled and all the amounts already remitted by 
him will be confiscated to Government. The tree 
growth will then be re- auctioned at his risk and 
loss. If any loss occurs to Government on account 
of the re-auction , it will be recovered from the 
originalbidder . 

11. The bidder shall obtain a license from 
the Divisional Forest Officer, Shencottah before 
commencement of the work . 

12. The contract shall not be transferred 
without cbtaining prior sanction of the competent 
authority . 

13. The tree growth may be removed 
either as round logs, sawn materials or as fire 
wood or as charcoal as the contractor chooses. 

14. The purchaser will have to fell, collect 
and remove the products from the area within 
one month from date of order to start work or 
from date of agreement whichever is earlier. 

15. All the produce found uncollected or 
unremoved from the area at the expiry of the 
contractperiod shall be forfeited to Government, 
but the Chief Conservator of Forests shall have 
power to give extension of time subject to en 
forcement of penalties according to the merits 
of the contract. 

16 . The successful bidder should produce 
certificate for the clearance of income- tax and 
sales-tax due to Government. Removal of the 
produce from the area will be permitted only on 
receipt of the above certificates. 

17. The sale is subject to rules regarding 
sales tax and the sale -tax amount should be paid 
as soon as confirmation of the sale is communi 
cated . 

18. Further particulars, if any, required 
can be had from the office of the undersigned or 
the Range Officer , Shendurney . The draft copy 
of agreement form is available in the Divisional 
Forest Office, Shencottah . The bidders may get 
themselves acquainted with the conditions of the 
agreement before partaking in the auction and 
the complaints regarding agreement received 
afterwards will not be entertained on any 
account

TRAVANCORE-COCHIN GAZETTE 


PART III 


KONI DIVISION .

NOTICES . 
(1) 

: 29th February 1956. 
Tenders are invited for collection , purchase 
and removal of the undernoted quantities of logs 
and saplings with the additional quantities avai 
lable in each class under mel-labhom system 
from Naduvathumuzhi Teak Plantations where 
thinnings have been carried out during 1954-55 . 

2. Tenders should reach the Divisional 
Forest Officer, koni before 3 P. M. on 5-5-1956 
together with a receipled chalan for Rs. 200 being 
earnest money deposited under Revenue Deposit 
in Pathanamthitta sub Treasury . Chalan for the 
remittance of earnest money can be had from this 
Office . 

3. Tenders should quote uniform percentage 
of standard rates shown below . 

4. Tenders willbe opened by the Divisional 
Forest Officer at 3-30 P. M. on the same date. 
The successful tenderer should pay one third 
value of the minimum quantity calculated at the 
tendered rate on the spot failing which the 
earnestmoney deposited by him will be forfeited 
to theGovernment and the right will be resold 
at his risk and loss and he will have no gain if 
any resulting from the transactions. The balance 
value of the minimum quantity should be paid up 
within 30 days from the date of agreement or 
permission to start work whichever is earlier as 
per chalan issued by the Teak Plantation Ollicer. 
The minimum quantity that should be collected 
and removed after payment of value at the 
offered rates and their departmental standard 
rates are given in the schedule appended . If 
collected or not the successful tenderer should 
pay the value of the minimum quantity at the 
agreed rates. Failure to pay for the minimum 
number within the specified time will entail for 
feiture of the security deposit and recovery of 
the due amount under the provisions of the 
Revenue Recovery Act in force . The value of 
the excess quantity if any collected should be 
paid within 15 days from the date of chalan 
issued by the Teak Plantation Officer . A fine at 
rupee one per day will be charged for belated 
payment of this amount till the expiry of the next 
thirty days after which date the contract will be 
cancelled and all the produce collected will be 
resold at his risk and loss... 

5. The successful tenderer will have to 
remit the security calculated at 10 per centof the 
contract value and execute the agreement within 
15 days from the date of intimation of the confir 
mation of the tender. In the event of his failure 
to do so, he shall have to pay penal interest at 
12 per cent per annum for the next. 15 days after 
which the sale will be cancelled and all the 
amount remitted by him will be forfeited to 
Government. He shall produce valid clearance 
certificates of sales tax without which remorals 
of the produce will not be allowed if the contract 
value exceeds Rs. 5,000. 

6. All produce must be collected and listed 
out within 60 days from the date of agreement or 
permission to start work which ever is earlier. 
Timemay however be allowed if required for 
the removals of the produce from the Teak Plan 
tation up to the end of December 1956. 

7. The contractor will have to pay seignio- . 
rage of vakka at the existing rates. 

8. The logs and saplings should be got 
checkmeasured and removed as per pass issued 
by the Teak Plantation Oficer, Neduvathumuzhi.

3rd APRIL . 1956 ) 


DEPARTMENT OF INDUSTRIES 


49 


NOTIFICATION 
No. G2-14 /56 . 

23rd March 1956. 
(EXAMINATION IN MALAYALAM SHORTHAND 

AND TYPEWRITING - APRIL-MAY 1956.) 
1. The Examination in Malayalam Shorthand 
and Typewriting will be conducted by the Board 
of Examiners in April-May 1956. 

2. The Examinations will be held at Trivan 
drum only during the year. The order of time 
held will be published in the Government Gazette 
sufficiently in advance of the Examinations. 

3. The Examinations will be of two grades , 
Lower and Higher. The number of papers in 
each grade on the subject, the maximum marks, 

the minimum required for passing etc. will be 
* -specified in the syllabus. 

4. QUALIFICATION OF CANDIDATES , 

( A ) Lower Grade. Candidates for admis 
sion to the Lower Grade Examinations must be 
persons (1 ) who have passed the Malayalam 
Higher or E. S.L. C. Examination ( 2) persons 
who have previously appeared for the examina 
tion in the subject.. 

(B ) Higher Grade. Candidates for admis 
sion to the Higher Grade Examination must be : 
(1) persons who have passed the Malayalam 
(Higher) or E. S. L. C. Examinations (2 ) persons 
who have passed the Lower grade Examination 
in the subject. 

5. The following is the scale of fees laid down 
aor admission to the Examination . 
Lower grade Examination Rs. 5 for each 

subject. 
Higher grade Examination Rs. 10 for each 

subject. 
6. Candidates must send in their application 
for admission to the examinations, to the Director 
of Industries and Commerce, Trivandrum so as 
to reach him on or before Saturday, the 21st 
April 1956. Applications may be received for a 
period of 10 days after the 21st April if accom 
panied by a penalty of Re. 1 per candidate . The 
applicationsmust be on printed forms which may 
be obtained free from the Director of Industries 
and Commerce. 


7. Every applications should be accompanied 
by satisfactory proof of the applicant s qualifica 
tion by virtue of which he is eligible to appear 
for the examination in the grade applied for and 
duly signed by the Manager, Sankethika Vidya 
Mandiram , Trivandrum . 

8. The prescribed fees must be paid in every 
case into a Government treasury under Head of 
Account XXXII Industries & Supplies (a ) Indus 
tries (9 ) other items and the treasury officer s 
receiptmust be attached to the application of the 
candidate . 

9. The fees once paid will not be refunded nor 
will it be available for a subsequent examination , 
Candidates must therefore assure themselves 
before paying their fees that they have one or 
other of the qualifications prescribed for the 
grade of the examination that they apply for as 
per rule 4 supra. 

10. Only one form of application must be used 
by each candidate and only one centre selected 
by him though hemay be applying for more than 
one subject. 

11. A candidate can appear for only one grade 
of a subject in any year. 

12. Candidates will be supplied with the paper 
necessary for answering the examinations . They 
should provide themselves with their own pens, 
pencils, ink, erasers etc. required . Candidates 
in Shorthand should bring their own ruled papers 
for taking down shorthand dictations. Candidates 
in Typewriting must bring their own typewriters 
and the necessary carbon papers, pencils ,pens, 
rulers, erasers etc. 

13. The , Typewriting Examination will, if 
necessary, be conducted in batches not exceed 
ing four. 

14. The names of successful candidates for 
each subject arranged in two classes, first and 
second will be published in the Gazette. To each 
successful candidate a certificate will be given 
stating the subject in which he has passed ,and 
the class in which he has been placed . 

15. Application from or on behalf of a success 
ful candidate asking for information as to the 
cause of failure or for a revaluation of answer 
papers will not be attended to . 

P. V. NAIR , 
Trivandrum . 

Director of Industries and 

Commerce.

Gazette No. 14 dated 3rd April 1956. 
Part II 


NOTIFICATION 
The following Bye-laws made by the Municipal Council, 
Shertallai under Section 12 of the Travancore -Cochin Local 
Authorities Entertainment Tax Act VI of 1951 and approved 
by Government in Order R. Dis .No. 26784 /55/ DD dated 
28-2-1956 are hereby published for the information of the 
public. 

CHAPTER I 
Definitions: 
1. For the purpose of these byelaws, 

( a) Entertainment shall include a series of entertainments 
held on any one day, reckoned from sunrise to sunrise. 

(b ) Act means, the Travancore-Cochin Local Authorities 
Entertainment tax Act VI of 1951. 

(c ) Executive Authority means the Executive Authority 
appointed under the Travancore-District Municipalities Act 
XXIII of 1116 . 

CHAPTER II 
2. The Proprietor of an entertainment or series of entertain 
ments shall, 

(a ) not less than 3 days before the commencement of the 
entertainment or the first of such series of entertainment if 
they are to be held for notmore than seven days in the aggre 
gate and, 

(b ) not less than seven days before the commencement of 
the first series of entertainment if they are to be held for more 
than 7 days in the aggregate , 
intimate in writing to the Executive Authority about the 
holding of the entertainment or such series of entertainment 
specifying the time and place for the conduct thereof and shall 
also furnish such other particulars regarding the duration of 
the entertainment, the class accommodation and payment of 
admission thereto and the manner in which the admissions 
will be checked . 

Provided that the Executive Authority may for sufficient 
reasonsto be recorded in writing condone the delay in present 
ing the application referred to above if the application is made 
at least 24 hours before the commencement of 1st of the 
series of entertainments. 

3. The Executive Authority may issue such directions as he 
deems fit in respect of the time and place of the holding of the 
entertainmentand the supervision thereof. 

G.976 .

NOTICE 

- 14th March 1956 . 
A dead Konna tree (BG . No. 50) standing in 
the Palace Guards area at Trivandrum , will be 
sold in public auction at the spot at 1100 hrs. on 
4-4-1956 . 


The successfulbidder will deposit the whole 
amount of the bid soon after the knock down and 
remove the tree within two days. 

If any successful bidder fails to deposit the 
bid amount on the spot and remove the tree , it 
will be reauctioned and the loss sustained if any
